## Reasoning

The champion has score -1.66, which is significantly better than everything else (all others score 0.00 or worse). The champion uses:
- State 0: GoAwayColor (cyan=6) → moves away from cyan walls, transitions when neighbors above threshold 8
- State 1: GoToColor (yellow=4) → moves toward yellow objects, 4 transitions including black floor detection
- State 2: Attraction (gain 2.74) with yellow LED, transitions on white floor or gray floor
- State 3: Attraction (gain 1.09) with cyan LED, transitions on neighbor count or fixed probability

The key insight: GoAwayColor from cyan (=blue walls, one side of source) helps avoid bad items. GoToColor yellow seems to be navigating. The attraction states help with clustering/delivery.

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion verbatim (elitism)
2. Exploit: small edits to champion parameters (probabilities, gains, thresholds)
3. Explore: different topologies focusing on the foraging task - go to source, pick up item (black floor detection), return to nest (white floor detection), using color cues

Key colors: clr=1(red)=nest wall, clr=4(yellow)=?, clr=5(magenta)=?, clr=6(cyan)=blue walls (bad side)

For foraging: robots should go to black area (source), avoid bad half, return to white area (nest). The green walls (clr=2) are the good side, blue walls (clr=6) are the bad side (or vice versa - we don't know which decreases score).

The champion uses GoAwayColor cyan (6) as state 0 and GoToColor yellow (4) as state 1. Since cyan likely corresponds to the blue-walled bad half, avoiding cyan makes sense.

## Reasoning

The champion (-8.49) is a massive leap from #2 (-1.63). Let me analyze the champion:
- State 0: GoAwayColor(cyan=6) → if neighbors>8 go to state 1, if perceives green(2) go to state 0 (self-loop? No - wait, n0x1 destination 0 means "other state index 0" which with 4 states leaving state 0 maps to state 1... wait let me re-read)

Actually with 4 states, leaving state 0, the others are [1,2,3], so D=0→state1, D=1→state2, D=2→state3.
- State 0: GoAwayColor(cyan=6), LED yellow; transition 0: D=1→state1 if neighbors>8(w=10); transition 1: D=0→state1... wait D=0 from state 0 maps to state 1.

Let me re-read: "With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2." So from state I, other states are listed in order excluding I.

From state 0: others = [1,2,3], D=0→1, D=1→2, D=2→3
From state 1: others = [0,2,3], D=0→0, D=1→2, D=2→3
From state 2: others = [0,1,3], D=0→0, D=1→1, D=2→3
From state 3: others = [0,1,2], D=0→0, D=1→1, D=2→2

Champion analysis:
- S0: GoAwayColor(cyan/blue walls), transitions: if neighbors>8 → S1; if perceives green(2) → S1
- S1: GoToColor(yellow=4), transitions: if neighbors>6 → S2; if black floor → S1(D=1=S2? No D=1 from S1 = S2); black floor p=0.8 → D=1=S2... wait n1x1 D=1 from state 1 = state 2; n1x2 D=0 from state 1 = state 0; so gray floor → S0
- S2: Attraction(2.7), transitions: white floor → S0; gray floor → S1
- S3: GoToColor(green=2), transitions: black floor → S1; white floor → S2... wait n3x0 D=0 from S3 = S0, n3x1 D=1 from S3 = S1

Wait the champion: s3 GoToColor(green=2), n3x0 D=0→S0 if black floor p=0.8; n3x1 D=1→S1 if white floor p=0.3.

But S3 is never reached from any other state in the champion! S0→S1, S1→S2/S0, S2→S0/S1. State 3 is unreachable. So the champion is effectively 3 states.

The key pattern: GoAwayColor(cyan) → GoToColor(yellow) → Attraction → loop back. The green-detecting transition in S0 creates a loop.

The big insight: green=2 walls are the GOOD source side. The champion goes toward yellow items and avoids cyan walls (bad side). When it picks up from green side (good), returns.

For exploitation, I should:
1. Keep champion exactly
2. Vary the key parameters: neighbor thresholds, probabilities, attraction gains
3. Add state 3 as reachable (connect it)
4. Try making state 3 reachable and useful

Key things to try:
- Make S3 (GoToColor green) reachable from S2 (when white floor = at nest, go back to source via green side)
- Vary the neighbor thresholds (8→7, 8→9)
- Vary the color perception probability (0.5→0.3, 0.5→0.7)
- Try GoToColor(red=1) for nest navigation
- Try different attraction gains

## Reasoning

The results are very encouraging - the entire population is now scoring around -8, with the champion at -8.49. The topology is clearly converging: GoAwayColor(cyan) → GoToColor(yellow/green) → Attraction → GoToColor(green/red). All variants score between -6 and -8.49.

Key observations:
1. The champion structure works very well: S0=GoAwayColor(cyan), S1=GoToColor(yellow), S2=Attraction, S3=GoToColor(green)
2. The top variant (#1) adds a green-perception transition in S2 (GoToColor green wall detection), scoring -8.23
3. The variant with GoToColor(red) in S3 (#7) also scores well (-8.05)
4. The variant with GoToColor(red) in S2 (#2) scores -8.23
5. The neighbor threshold variations (7-9) all score similarly (-7.95 to -8.14)

The gap between the champion (-8.49) and everything else is small, suggesting we're near a local optimum but should keep exploiting.

**Exploitation focus:**
- Fine-tune the champion's key parameters: neighbor thresholds (8±1), sigmoid steepness (10±2, 14±2), probabilities
- Try combining the best features: add green-perception in S2 (from #1) + keep champion S3
- Try varying the perception probability (0.5→ other values)
- Try S3=GoToColor(red=1) for nest navigation

**Exploration:**
- Try making S3 reachable (add transition from S2 to S3)
- Try different steepness values for sigmoid conditions
- Try S1=GoToColor(green=2) instead of yellow=4
- Try adding more transitions to break out of loops

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

We're stuck at -8.49 for 3 rounds. The population is tightly clustered around -8.0 to -8.4. The topology is almost identical across all machines. Time for more aggressive exploration while keeping the champion.

Key observations:
1. Champion (-8.49): S0=GoAwayColor(cyan), S1=GoToColor(yellow), S2=Attraction, S3=GoToColor(green)
2. Best new variants keep the same structure with minor tweaks
3. Adding green perception in S1 (#1, -8.40) or S2 (#3, -8.12) helps somewhat
4. The "GoToColor(red) in S2" variant (#2, -8.31) consistently performs well

The scores have plateaued. I need to:
1. Try bolder parameter changes: very different neighbor thresholds (3-5 instead of 6-8), different sigmoid steepness
2. Try making S3 actually reachable from S2 via a transition
3. Try S1=GoToColor(green=2) as main foraging, using green walls to navigate to good source
4. Try GoAwayColor(cyan) more aggressively with lower threshold
5. Try S0=GoToColor(green=2) directly (go to good source) instead of GoAwayColor(cyan)
6. Combine features: S1 with green perception + S2 with green perception

The key insight I may be missing: the champion's S3 (GoToColor green) is unreachable. What if we made it reachable from S2 when at white floor? Currently S2→S0 on white, S2→S1 on gray. What if S2→S3 on white (go to green=good source after delivering)?

Also: try completely different topology - use floor color more explicitly for foraging cycle: black→carrying, white→delivered, gray→traveling.

## Reasoning

Still stuck at -8.62 champion. The population is tightly clustered around -8.0 to -8.4. The structure is very stable: GoAwayColor(cyan)→GoToColor(yellow)→GoToColor(red)→Attraction.

Key observations from round 7:
1. w1x0=16 (#1, -8.37) is slightly better than 14
2. att3=2.5 and att3=2.0 both score -8.21
3. p1x0=7 (#4, -8.21) same as p1x0=6
4. The champion uses w1x0=14, p1x0=6 - maybe try w1x0=16 combined with champion's other params
5. p2x0=0.9 seems optimal (better than 0.85 or 0.95)

The champion hasn't been beaten in 2 rounds. The topology seems locked. Let me think about what else to try:

**Exploitation:**
- Combine w1x0=16 with p1x0=6 (was tried, scored -8.37) - keep this
- Try w1x0=18, w1x0=20
- Try p1x0=5 with w1x0=16
- Try p2x0=0.9 with different p2x1 (0.2, 0.4)
- Try att3=2.0 with p3x0=0.1/0.2
- Try adding green-wall perception (l=2) to champion

**Exploration:**
- Try completely different w values for sigmoid
- Try 3-state version of the champion topology
- Try S0 with lower neighbor threshold (6,7)
- Try changing S0's GoAwayColor to GoToColor(green=2) directly

The key: w0x0=10 in S0 means the sigmoid for neighbor threshold 8 is very steep. What if we vary this?

## Reasoning

The champion is now -8.65, a marginal improvement. The champion uses w1x0=14 (not 16), p1x1=0.85, p1x2=0.55 - the exact same as before. The w1x0=16 variants consistently score around -8.00 to -8.28, slightly worse than the champion's 14.

Key observations:
1. Champion: w1x0=14, p1x1=0.85, p1x2=0.55, p2x0=0.9, p2x1=0.3, p2x2=0.5, att3=2.0, p3x0=0.15, p3x1=0.5
2. The #2 machine (-8.28): w1x0=16, p1x2=0.45, p2x2=0.55, p3x1=0.45 - slightly different
3. The #3 machine (-8.26): w1x0=16, p2x1=0.2 
4. w1x0=16 is consistently worse than w1x0=14 for the champion structure

With only 2 rounds left, I need to:
1. Keep champion (mandatory)
2. Focus tight exploitation around champion parameters
3. Try small variations: w1x0=12/13/15, p1x1=0.80/0.90, p1x2=0.50/0.60, p2x0=0.85/0.95, p2x1=0.25/0.35, att3=1.5/2.5/3.0
4. A few explorations: add perception transitions, try different S3

The champion has w1x0=14 which is better than 16, 18. Maybe try 12, 13. The p2x1=0.3 (black floor→S1) seems important - what about 0.2 or 0.4? The att3=2.0 is what we haven't varied much in the champion topology.

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

The arena contains a nest area at one end, with a WHITE floor, and a source area at the opposite end, with a BLACK floor. The floor in between is GRAY. The wall on the nest side is RED.

The source area is divided into two halves. The wall blocks behind one half are BLUE and those behind the other half are GREEN.

A robot carries at most one item. Entering a half of the source gives the robot an item of that half's type, replacing any item it was already carrying. Entering the nest with an item delivers it: an item from one half decreases the score by 1, an item from the other half increases it by 1.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
